## B.2 Impact of Product Fit Uncertainty on Developers’ Profits

Proposition B2 summarizes how product fit uncertainty $\alpha _ { k }$ affects the developers’ profits in each case.<sup>29</sup>

## Proposition B2: Product fit uncertainty and profits

As product fit uncertainty $\alpha _ { k }$ decreases,

(i) the profit of Developer 1 in Case PH increases if Whole App 1’s quality is relatively low $( v _ { 1 } < v _ { 2 b } + t -$ $\frac { \alpha _ { P H } ( { \bar { t } } + t _ { b } ) + n _ { 2 } { \bar { c } } } { 2 } )$ and decreases otherwise.

(ii) The profit of Developer 2 in Case PH increases if Whole App 2’s quality is relatively moderate $( \underline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } < v _ { 2 } <$ $\overline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } )$ and one of the following two conditions hold: (a) Basic App 2’s quality is relatively high $( v _ { 2 b } > v _ { 2 b } ^ { P H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively low $( a < a _ { 2 } ^ { P H } )$ , (b) Basic App 2’s quality is relatively low $( v _ { 2 b } < v _ { 2 b } ^ { P H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively high $( a > a _ { 2 } ^ { P H } )$ ; otherwise, it decreases.

(iii) The profit of developer ?? in Case HH increases if whole app ??’s quality is relatively moderate $( \underline { { v } } _ { i } ^ { H H } < v _ { i } <$ $\overline { { v } } _ { i } ^ { H H } )$ and one of the following two conditions hold: (a) basic app ??’s quality is relatively high $( v _ { i b } > v _ { i b } ^ { H H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively low $( a < a _ { i } ^ { H H } )$ ; (b) basic app ??’s quality is relatively low $( v _ { i b } < v _ { i b } ^ { H H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively high $( a > a _ { i } ^ { H H } )$ ; otherwise it decreases. The expressions of $\underline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } , \overline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } , \ v _ { 2 b } ^ { P H }$ $a _ { 2 } ^ { P H } , \underline { { { v } } } _ { 1 } ^ { H H } , \overline { { { v } } } _ { 1 } ^ { H H } , v _ { 1 b } ^ { H H } , a _ { 1 } ^ { H H } , \underline { { { v } } } _ { 2 } ^ { H H } , \overline { { { v } } } _ { 2 } ^ { H H } , v _ { 2 b } ^ { H H }$ and $a _ { 2 } ^ { H H }$ are presented in the detailed proofs below.

Similar to the main model, the one offering a paid whole app in Case PH favors lower product fit uncertainty when its whole app’s quality is relatively low, but prefers higher product fit uncertainty when its whole app’s quality is relatively high. However, for developers adopting the hybrid strategy in Case PH or Case HH, the impact of product fit uncertainty on their profit is different from that in the main model. Specifically, the corresponding results are reorganized in Table B1.

Table B1. Change of Developer ??’s Profit under the Hybrid Strategy as Product Fit Uncertainty Decreases

<table><tr><td rowspan="2">No.</td><td colspan="3">Conditions</td><td rowspan="2">Change of developer i&#x27;s profit</td></tr><tr><td>Whole app i&#x27;s quality (vi)</td><td>Basic app i&#x27;s quality (vib)</td><td>Ad revenue rate (a)</td></tr><tr><td>(i)</td><td>low</td><td>-</td><td>-</td><td>decreases</td></tr><tr><td>(ii)</td><td>moderate</td><td>high</td><td>high</td><td>decreases</td></tr><tr><td>(iii)</td><td>moderate</td><td>low</td><td>high</td><td>increases</td></tr><tr><td>(iv)</td><td>moderate</td><td>high</td><td>low</td><td>increases</td></tr><tr><td>(v)</td><td>moderate</td><td>low</td><td>low</td><td>decreases</td></tr><tr><td>(vi)</td><td>high</td><td>-</td><td>-</td><td>decreases</td></tr></table>

Although the above results seem to be different from those in the main model, the rationales behind are the same. Consistent with the main model, the app quality plays an important role in the impact of product fit uncertainty on the developer’s profits. Since the developer adopting the hybrid strategy earns from both the whole app users and basic app users, the impact of product fit uncertainty on the developer’s profit depends on both the whole app’s quality and basic app’s quality. As product fit uncertainty decreases, the profit of selling in-app purchases increases when the whole app’s quality is relatively low but decreases when the whole app’s quality is relatively high. Similarly, the profit of selling ads increases when the basic app’s quality is relatively low but decreases when the basic app’s quality is relatively high.

In Condition (i), the whole app’s quality is relatively low. That is, the whole app’s price would be relatively low and the basic app’s quality is relatively high. With a relatively low price of the whole app, the product fit uncertainty’s impact on the profit of selling in-app purchases is dominated by its impact on the profit of selling ads. With relatively high quality of the basic app, the profit of selling ads decreases with a lower product fit uncertainty. To conclude, as product fit uncertainty decreases, the reduction in the profit of selling ads dominates, thus the developer’s profit goes down.

In Condition (vi), the whole app’s quality is relatively high, which implies that the whole app’s price would be relatively high. With a relatively high price of the whole app, the product fit uncertainty’s impact on the profit of selling in-app purchases dominates its impact on the profit of selling ads. With relatively high quality of the whole app, the profit of selling in-app purchases decreases with a lower product fit uncertainty. To conclude, as product fit uncertainty decreases, the reduction in the profit of selling in-app purchases dominates, thus the developer’s profit goes down.

In Conditions (ii)-(v), the whole app’s quality is relatively moderate. Thus, the impact of product fit uncertainty on the profit of selling either in-app purchases or ads may dominate, depending on the ad revenue rate.

When the ad revenue rate is relatively high in conditions (ii)-(iii), the product fit uncertainty’s impact on the profit of selling ads dominates its impact on the profit of selling in-app purchases. In Condition (ii), the basic app’s quality is relatively high. As product fit uncertainty reduces, the profit of selling ads decreases, thus the developer’s profit goes down. In Condition (iii), the basic app’s quality is relatively low. As product fit uncertainty reduces, the profit of selling ads increases, thus the developer’s profit goes up.

When the ad revenue rate is relatively low in Conditions (iv)-(v), the product fit uncertainty’s impact on the profit of selling ads is dominated by its impact on the profit of selling in-app purchases. In Condition (iii), the basic app’s quality is relatively high, i.e., the whole app’s quality is close to the lower bound of the moderate range. As product fit uncertainty reduces, the profit of selling in-app purchases increases, thus the developer’s profit goes up. In Condition (v), the basic app’s quality is relatively low, i.e., the whole app’s quality is close to the upper bound of the moderate range. As product fit uncertainty reduces, the profit of selling in-app purchases decreases, thus the developer’s profit goes down.

## Detailed proofs of Proposition B2 are given below:

$\frac { \partial \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } } { \partial \alpha _ { P H } } = \frac { - ( \alpha _ { P H } ( t + t _ { b } ) - 2 t + 2 v _ { 1 } - 2 v _ { 2 b } + n _ { 2 } \vec { c } ) ( \alpha _ { P H } ( t + t _ { b } ) - 2 t _ { b } - 2 v _ { 1 } + 2 v _ { 2 b } - n _ { 2 } \vec { c } ) } { 1 6 ( t + t _ { b } ) ( 1 - \alpha _ { P H } ) ^ { 2 } } .$ With $x _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P H } > 0$ for $c \in [ 0 , \bar { c } ]$ , we have $v _ { 2 b } < v _ { 1 } +$ $\frac { ( 1 - \alpha _ { P H } ) ( t + t _ { b } ) - ( t - t _ { b } ) - n _ { 2 } \bar { c } } { 2 }$ . That is, $\alpha _ { P H } ( t + t _ { b } ) - 2 t _ { b } - 2 v _ { 1 } + 2 v _ { 2 b } - n _ { 2 } \bar { c } < 0$ . Thus, $\frac { \partial \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } } { \partial \alpha _ { P H } } < 0$ if $v _ { 1 } < v _ { 2 b } + t -$ $\frac { \alpha _ { P H } ( t + t _ { b } ) + n _ { 2 } \bar { c } } { 2 } ,$ and $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } } { \partial \alpha _ { P H } } > 0 \mathrm { ~ i f ~ } v _ { 1 } > v _ { 2 b } + t - \frac { \alpha _ { P H } ( t + t _ { b } ) + n _ { 2 } \bar { c } } { 2 } } \end{array}$ . Therefore, as product fit uncertainty $\alpha _ { P H }$ decreases, Developer 1’s profit increases if Whole App 1’s quality is relatively low $\begin{array} { r } { ( v _ { 1 } < v _ { 2 b } + t - \frac { \alpha _ { P H } ( t + t _ { b } ) + n _ { 2 } \bar { c } } { 2 } ) } \end{array}$ and decreases otherwise.

Solving $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P H } } { \partial \alpha _ { P H } } = 0 } \end{array}$ , we have $\frac { \partial \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P H } } { \partial \alpha _ { P H } } < 0$ if one of the following two conditions hold: $( 1 ) { \underline { { v } } } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } < v _ { 2 } < { \overline { { v } } } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } , v _ { 2 b } > v _ { 2 b } ^ { P H }$ and $a < a _ { 2 } ^ { P H }$ , (2) $\begin{array} { r } { \underline { { v _ { 2 } ^ { P H } } } < v _ { 2 } < \overline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } , v _ { 2 b } < v _ { 2 b } ^ { P H } } \end{array}$ and $a > a _ { 2 } ^ { P H }$ , where $\begin{array} { r } { \underline { v } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } = a n _ { 2 } + v _ { 2 b } + \frac { t - t _ { b } - n _ { 2 } \bar { c } } { 2 } - } \end{array}$

$$
\begin{array}{r l r} & {\frac {\sqrt {(1 - \alpha_ {P H}) ^ {2} (t ^ {2} - t _ {b} ^ {2}) ^ {2} + 4 a n _ {2} (t ^ {2} - t _ {b} ^ {2}) (n _ {2} \bar {c} - t + t _ {b} + 2 v _ {1} - 2 v _ {2 b})}}{2 (t + t _ {b})}} & {, \qquad \overline {{{v}}} _ {2} ^ {P H} = a n _ {2} + v _ {2 b} + \frac {t - t _ {b} - n _ {2} \bar {c}}{2} -} \\ & {\frac {\sqrt {(1 - \alpha_ {P H}) ^ {2} (t ^ {2} - t _ {b} ^ {2}) ^ {2} + 4 a n _ {2} (t ^ {2} - t _ {b} ^ {2}) (n _ {2} \bar {c} - t + t _ {b} + 2 v _ {1} - 2 v _ {2 b)})}}{2 (t + t _ {b})}, \quad v _ {2 b} ^ {P H} = v _ {1} - \frac {t - t _ {b} - n _ {2} \bar {c}}{2}, \quad \mathrm{and} \quad a _ {2} ^ {P H} = \frac {(1 - \alpha_ {P H}) ^ {2} (t ^ {2} - t _ {b} ^ {2})}{4 (t - t _ {b} - n _ {2} \bar {c} - 2 (v _ {1} - v _ {2 b}))}.} \end{array}
$$

Otherwise, $\frac { \partial \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P H } } { \partial \alpha _ { P H } } > 0$ . Thus, as product fit uncertainty $\alpha _ { P H }$ decreases, Developer 2’s profit increases if Whole App 2’s quality is relatively moderate $( \underline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } < v _ { 2 } < \overline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { P H } )$ and one of the following two conditions hold: (a) Basic App $2 \mathit { \ ' } _ { \mathbf { S } }$ quality is relatively high $( v _ { 2 b } > v _ { 2 b } ^ { P H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively low $( a < a _ { 2 } ^ { P H } )$ , (b) Basic App $2 \mathit { \ ' } _ { \mathbf { S } }$ quality is relatively low $( v _ { 2 b } < v _ { 2 b } ^ { P H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively high $( a > a _ { 2 } ^ { P H } )$ ; otherwise, it decreases.

Solving $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } } { \partial \alpha _ { H H } } = 0 } \end{array}$ , we have $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } } { \partial \alpha _ { H H } } < 0 } \end{array}$ if one of the following two conditions hold: (1) $\underline { { v } } _ { 1 } ^ { H H } < v _ { 1 } < \overline { { v } } _ { 1 } ^ { H H } , v _ { 1 b } > v _ { 1 b } ^ { H H }$ and $a < a _ { 1 } ^ { H H } , ( 2 )  \underline { { { v } } } _ { 1 } ^ { H H } < v _ { 1 } < \overline { { { v } } } _ { 1 } ^ { H H } , v _ { 1 b } < v _ { 1 b } ^ { H H }$ and $a > a _ { 1 } ^ { H H }$ , where $\begin{array} { r } { \underline { \boldsymbol { v } } _ { 1 } ^ { H H } = a \boldsymbol { n } _ { 1 } + \boldsymbol { v } _ { 1 b } + \frac { t - t _ { b } - n _ { 1 } \bar { \boldsymbol { c } } } { 2 } - } \end{array}$

$$
\begin{array}{r l r} & {\frac {\sqrt {t _ {b} ^ {2} (t - t _ {b}) ^ {2} (1 - \alpha_ {H H}) ^ {2} + 4 a n _ {1} t _ {b} (t - t _ {b}) (n _ {1} \bar {c} - n _ {2} \bar {c} - 2 v _ {1 b} + 2 v _ {2 b})}}{2 t _ {b}}} & {, \qquad \overline {{{v}}} _ {1} ^ {H H} = a n _ {1} + v _ {1 b} + \frac {t - t _ {b} - n _ {1} \bar {c}}{2} +} \\ & {\frac {\sqrt {t _ {b} ^ {2} (t - t _ {b}) ^ {2} (1 - \alpha_ {H H}) ^ {2} + 4 a n _ {1} t _ {b} (t - t _ {b}) (n _ {1} \bar {c} - n _ {2} \bar {c} - 2 v _ {1 b} + 2 v _ {\mathrm{2b}})}}{2 t _ {b}}, v _ {1 b} ^ {H H} = v _ {2 b} + \frac {(n _ {1} - n _ {2}) \bar {c}}{2}, \mathrm{and} a _ {1} ^ {H H} = \frac {t _ {b} (t - t _ {b}) (1 - \alpha_ {H H}) ^ {2}}{4 n _ {1} (2 v _ {1 b} - 2 v _ {\mathrm{2b}} - n _ {1} \bar {c} + n _ {2} \bar {c})}.} \end{array}
$$

Otherwise, $\frac { \partial \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } } { \partial \alpha _ { H H } } > 0$ . Thus, as product fit uncertainty $\alpha _ { H H }$ decreases, Developer 1’s profit increases if Whole App 1’s quality is relatively moderate $( \underline { { v } } _ { 1 } ^ { H H } < v _ { 1 } < \overline { { v } } _ { 1 } ^ { H H } )$ and one of the following two conditions hold: (a) Basic App 1’s quality is relatively high $( v _ { 1 b } > v _ { 1 b } ^ { H H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively low $( a < a _ { 1 } ^ { H H } )$ , (b) Basic App 1’s quality is relatively low $( v _ { 1 b } < v _ { 1 b } ^ { H H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively high $( a > a _ { 1 } ^ { H H } )$ ; otherwise it decreases.

Solving $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H H } } { \partial \alpha _ { H H } } = 0 } \end{array}$ , we have $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H H } } { \partial \alpha _ { H H } } < 0 } \end{array}$ if one of the following two conditions hold: $( 1 ) \ : \underline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { H H } < v _ { 2 } < \overline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { H H } , v _ { 2 b } > v _ { 2 b } ^ { H H }$ and $a < a _ { 2 } ^ { H H }$ , (2) $v _ { 2 } ^ { H H } < v _ { 2 } < \overline { { { v } } } _ { 2 } ^ { H H } , v _ { 2 b } < v _ { 2 b } ^ { H H }$ and $a > a _ { 2 } ^ { H H }$ , where $\begin{array} { r } { \underline { v } _ { 2 } ^ { H H } = a n _ { 2 } + v _ { 2 b } + \frac { t - t _ { b } - n _ { 2 } \bar { c } } { 2 } - } \end{array}$

$$
\begin{array}{r l r} & {\frac {\sqrt {t _ {b} ^ {2} (t - t _ {b}) ^ {2} (1 - \alpha_ {H H}) ^ {2} + 4 a n _ {2} t _ {b} (t - t _ {b}) (n _ {2} \bar {c} - n _ {1} \bar {c} - 2 v _ {2 b} + 2 v _ {1 b})}}{2 t _ {b}}} & {, \qquad \overline {{{v}}} _ {2} ^ {H H} = a n _ {2} + v _ {2 b} + \frac {t - t _ {b} - n _ {2} \bar {c}}{2} +} \\ & {\frac {\sqrt {t _ {b} ^ {2} (t - t _ {b}) ^ {2} (1 - \alpha_ {H H}) ^ {2} + 4 a n _ {2} t _ {b} (t - t _ {b}) (n _ {2} \bar {c} - n _ {1} \bar {c} - 2 v _ {2 b} + 2 v _ {\mathrm{1b}})}}{2 t _ {b}}, v _ {2 b} ^ {H H} = v _ {1 b} + \frac {(n _ {2} - n _ {1}) \bar {c}}{2}, \mathrm{and} a _ {2} ^ {H H} = \frac {t _ {b} (t - t _ {b}) (1 - \alpha_ {H H}) ^ {2}}{4 n _ {2} (2 v _ {2 b} - 2 v _ {\mathrm{1b}} - n _ {2} \bar {c} + n _ {1} \bar {c})}.} \end{array}
$$

Otherwise, $\frac { \partial \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H H } } { \partial \alpha _ { H H } } > 0$ . Thus, as product fit uncertainty $\alpha _ { H H }$ decreases, Developer 2’s profit increases if Whole App 2’s quality is relatively moderate $( \underline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { H H } < v _ { 2 } < \overline { { v } } _ { 2 } ^ { H H } )$ and one of the following two conditions hold: (a) Basic App 2’s quality is relatively high $( v _ { 2 b } > v _ { 2 b } ^ { H H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively low $( a < a _ { 2 } ^ { H H } )$ , (b) Basic App 2’s quality is relatively low $( v _ { 2 b } < v _ { 2 b } ^ { H H } )$ and the ad revenue rate is relatively high $( a > a _ { 2 } ^ { H H } )$ ; otherwise it decreases.

## B.3 Developers’ Equilibrium Strategies

## B.3.1 Impact of Ad Revenue Rate on Developers’ Equilibrium Strategies

We examine how the market equilibrium changes with the ad revenue rate in this subsection. The results are presented in Proposition B3.<sup>30</sup>

## Proposition B3: The ad revenue rate and market equilibria

As the ad revenue rate (??) increases,

(i) The equilibrium changes from Case PP to Case HP if CB1 holds.

(ii) The equilibrium changes from Case PH to Case HH if CB2 holds. The expressions of CB1 and CB2 are presented in the detailed proofs below.

Consistent with our intuition, as ad revenue rate increases, the developer benefits more from selling ads, thus the developer is more likely to adopt the hybrid strategy. As a result, a higher ad revenue rate may increase the number of free basic apps in the market.

## Detailed proofs of Proposition B3 are given below:

The profit functions of Developer 1 in Case PP and Case HP are $\pi _ { 1 } = p _ { 1 } d _ { 1 }$ and $\pi _ { 1 } = p _ { 1 } d _ { 1 } + a n _ { 1 } d _ { 1 b }$ , respectively. Thus, we have $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } } { \partial a } = 0 \mathrm { ~ a n d } \frac { \bar { \partial } \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } } { \partial a } > 0 } \end{array}$ . That is, $\frac { \partial ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } ) } { \partial a } < 0$ . Ceteris paribus, if ?? increases from ?? to ${ \overline { { a } } } .$ , the equilibrium changes from Case PP to Case HP when the equilibrium of $a = a$ is Case PP while that of $a = { \overline { { a } } }$ is Case HP. CB1 in the proposition is given by min $\left\{ ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } ) | _ { a = \underline { { { a } } } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P H } ) | _ { a = \underline { { { a } } } } , ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } ) | _ { a = \overline { { { a } } } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } ) \right\}$ $\pi _ { 2 } ^ { H H } ) | _ { a = \overline { { { a } } } } \} > 0$

The profit functions of Developer 1 in Case PH and Case HH are $\pi _ { 1 } = p _ { 1 } d _ { 1 }$ and $\pi _ { 1 } = p _ { 1 } d _ { 1 } + a n _ { 1 } d _ { 1 b }$ , respectively. Thus, we have $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } } { \partial a } = 0 } \end{array}$ and $\frac { \partial \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } } { \partial a } > 0$ . That is, $\frac { \partial ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } ) } { \partial a } < 0$ . Ceteris paribus, if ?? increases from ?? to ??, the equilibrium changes from Case PH to Case HH when the equilibrium of $a = \underline { { a } }$ is Case PH while that of $a = { \overline { { a } } }$ is Case HH. CB2 in the proposition is given by min $\left\{ ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } ) | _ { a = \underline { { { a } } } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P P } ) | _ { a = \underline { { { a } } } } , ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } ) | _ { a = \overline { { { a } } } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } ) \right\}$ $\pi _ { 2 } ^ { H P } ) | _ { a = \overline { { { a } } } } \} > 0$

## B.3.2 Impact of Basic App Quality on Developers’ Equilibrium Strategies

We analyze how the higher quality of Basic App 1 affects the market equilibrium in this subsection.<sup>31</sup> The results are summarized in Proposition B4.

## Proposition B4: Basic app quality and market equilibria

As the quality of Basic $\mathsf { A p p } \mathrm { 1 } ( v _ { 1 b } )$ increases,

(i) The equilibrium changes from Case PP to Case HP if CB3 holds, but changes from Case HP to Case PP if CB4 holds.

(ii) The equilibrium changes from Case PH to Case HH if CB5 holds, but changes from Case HH to Case PH if CB6 holds. The expressions of CB3, CB4, CB5 and CB6 are presented in the detailed proofs below.

Different from the results of the main model, Proposition B4 suggests that the higher quality of Basic App 1 may increase or decrease the number of free basic apps in the market. That is, in addition to the change from Case HP to Case PP or the change from Case HH to Case PH, a higher quality of Basic App 1 may also lead to the change from Case PP to Case HP or the change from Case PH to Case HH.

In Case PP or Case PH, Developer 1 offers ad-free paid apps, thus Developer 1’s profit would not be affected by the quality of Basic App 1. In Case HP or Case HH, as Basic App 1’s quality increases, the demand of Basic App 1 increases but that of Whole App 1 decreases. Similar to the main model, Developer 1 suffers more from the cannibalization effect and loses revenue from selling the in-app purchases. However, the developer adopting the hybrid strategy also earns ad revenue, thus Developer 1 may benefit from the larger size of Basic App 1. As a result, a higher quality of Basic App 1 may decrease or increase the profit of Developer 1 under the hybrid strategy, which may incentivize Developer 1 to offer an ad-free paid app or to adopt the hybrid strategy.

## Detailed proofs of Proposition B4 are given below:

$$
\frac {\partial \big (\pi_ {1} ^ {P P} - \pi_ {1} ^ {H P} \big)}{\partial v _ {1 b}} = \frac {n _ {1} \big ((t + t _ {b}) \overline {{c}} - 4 a t \big) - (t + t _ {b}) (t \alpha_ {H P} - t _ {b} \alpha_ {H P} - 2 v _ {1} + 2 v _ {1 b})}{4 (1 - \alpha_ {H P}) \big (t ^ {2} - t _ {b} ^ {2} \big)}.
$$

Ceteris paribus, if $v _ { 1 b }$ increases from $\underline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ to $\overline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ , the equilibrium changes from Case PP to Case HP when $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \big ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { \bar { H } P } \big ) } { \partial v _ { 1 b } } < 0 } \end{array}$ and the equilibrium of $v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ is Case PP while that of $v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ is Case HP. CB3 in the proposition is given by min $\begin{array} { r } { \left\{ - \frac { \partial \left( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } \right) } { \partial v _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } ) \big | _ { v _ { 1 b } = v _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P H } ) \big | _ { v _ { 1 b } = v _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } ) \right\} } \end{array}$ $\pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { { v } } } _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H P } - \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H H } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { { v } } } _ { 1 b } } \} > 0 .$

Ceteris paribus, if $v _ { 1 b }$ increases from $\underline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ to $\overline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ , the equilibrium changes from Case HP to Case PP when $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \left( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } \right) } { \partial v _ { 1 b } } > 0 } \end{array}$ and the equilibrium of $v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ is Case HP while that of $v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ is Case PP. CB4 in the proposition is given by min $\left\{ \frac { \partial ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } ) } { \partial v _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b } , } ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H P } - \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H H } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b } , } ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { v } } _ { 1 b } , } ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } ) \right\}$ $\pi _ { 2 } ^ { P H } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { { v } } } _ { 1 b } } \bigg \} > 0 .$

$$
\frac {\partial \left(\pi_ {1} ^ {P H} - \pi_ {1} ^ {H H}\right)}{\partial v _ {1 b}} = \frac {n _ {1} (t _ {b} \overline {{c}} - 2 a t) - t _ {b} (t \alpha_ {H H} - t _ {b} \alpha_ {H H} - 2 v _ {1} + 2 v _ {1 b})}{4 (1 - \alpha_ {H H}) (t - t _ {b}) t _ {b}}.
$$

Ceteris paribus, if $v _ { 1 b }$ increases from $\underline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ to $\overline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ , the equilibrium changes from Case PH to Case HH when $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \left( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } \right) } { \partial v _ { 1 b } } < 0 } \end{array}$ and the equilibrium of $v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ is Case PH while that of $v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ is Case HH. CB5 in the proposition is given by min $\begin{array} { r } { \left\{ { - \frac { \partial \left( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } \right) } { \partial v _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } ) \vert _ { v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P P } ) \vert _ { v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } ) \vert _ { v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b } } } \right\} } \end{array}$ $\pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { v } } _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H H } - \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H P } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { v } } _ { 1 b } } \} > 0 .$

Ceteris paribus, if $v _ { 1 b }$ increases from $\underline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ to $\overline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ , the equilibrium changes from Case HH to Case PH when $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial { \left( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { \bar { H } H } \right) } } { \partial v _ { 1 b } } > 0 } \end{array}$ and the equilibrium of $v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ is Case HH while that of $v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { v } } _ { 1 b }$ is Case PH. CB6 in the proposition is given by min $\left\{ \frac { \partial ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } ) } { \partial v _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H H } - \pi _ { 2 } ^ { H P } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H H } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { v } } _ { 1 b } } , ( \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P H } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P H } ) | _ { v _ { 2 b } = \overline { { v } } _ { 2 b } } \right\} = 0 .$ $\pi _ { 2 } ^ { P P } ) | _ { v _ { 1 b } = \overline { { { v } } } _ { 1 b } } \bigg \} > 0$

## B.3.3 Impact of Whole App Quality on Developers’ Equilibrium Strategies

We analyze how the higher quality of Whole App 1 affects the market equilibrium in this subsection.<sup>32</sup> The results are summarized in Proposition B5.

## Proposition B5: Whole app quality and market equilibria

As the quality of Whole $\mathrm { A p p } \ 1 \ ( v _ { 1 } )$ increases,

(i) The equilibrium changes from Case PP to Case HP if CB7 holds, but changes from Case HP to Case PP if CB8 holds.

(ii) The equilibrium changes from Case PH to Case HH if CB9 holds, but changes from Case HH to Case PH if CB10 holds. The expressions of CB7, CB8, CB9 and CB10 are presented in the detailed proofs below.

Although the role of product fit uncertainty in the impact of whole app’s quality on the market equilibrium is not that clear as the main model, we can analyze the results based on identical rationales in the main model.

Consistent with the main model, when the difference in product fit uncertainty between Case HP and Case PP is not taken into consideration, an improvement in Whole App 1’s quality has a greater positive impact on the potential market size of Whole $\mathbf { A p p }$ 1 in Case HP than that in Case PP. Similarly, an improvement in Whole App 1’s quality has a greater positive impact on the potential market size of Whole App 1 in Case HH than that in Case PH. However, offering more free trials in Case HP or Case HH reduces product fit uncertainty, which in turn weakens the difference of the above positive impacts.

Different from that under the freemium strategy, the developer’s profit under the hybrid strategy also depends on the demand of basic app in addition to that of whole app. An improvement in Whole App 1’s quality has a negative impact on the potential market size of Basic App 1 in Case HP or Case HH, which would be weakened by a larger reduction of product fit uncertainty.

In consequence, if offering more free trials significantly (slightly) reduces product fit uncertainty, an improvement in Whole App 1’s quality results in a minor (greater) positive impact on the potential market size of Whole $\mathrm { A p p }$ 1, but it also leads to a minor (greater) negative impact on the potential market size of Basic App 1. Therefore, the role of product fit uncertainty in the impact of whole app’s quality on the market equilibrium is not that clear as the main model.

Detailed proofs of Proposition B5 are given below:

$$
\begin{array}{r l} & {\frac {\partial (\pi_ {1} ^ {P P} - \pi_ {1} ^ {H P})}{\partial v _ {1}} = \frac {1}{3 6 t (t - t _ {b}) (1 - \alpha_ {H P}) (1 - \alpha_ {P P})} (\alpha_ {H P} (3 t \alpha_ {P P} - 3 t - 4 v _ {1} + 4 v _ {2}) (t - t _ {b}) - 3 t \alpha_ {P P} (6 a n _ {1} - 3 n _ {1} \overline {{c}} + 4 t - 4 t _ {b} - 4 v _ {1} + 4 v _ {2}) + 1 8 a n _ {1} t - 9 n _ {1} t \overline {{c}} + 1 2 t ^ {2} - 1 2 t t _ {b} - 1 4 t v _ {1} + 1 8 t v _ {1 b} - 4 t v _ {2} - 4 t _ {b} v _ {1} + 4 t _ {b} v _ {2}).} \end{array}
$$

Ceteris paribus, if $v _ { 1 }$ increases from $\underline { { v _ { 1 } } }$ to $\overline { { v } } _ { 1 }$ , the equilibrium changes from Case PP to Case HP when $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \partial \left( \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } - \pi _ { 1 } ^ { H P } \right) } { \partial v _ { 1 } } < 0 } \end{array}$ and the equilibrium of $v _ { 1 } = \underline { { v } } _ { 1 }$ is Case PP while that of $v _ { 1 } = \overline { { v } } _ { 1 }$ is Case HP. ## B.3.4 Impact of the Strength of Consumer Preference on Developers’ Equilibrium Strategies

We examine how the market equilibrium changes with the strength of consumer preference in this subsection. The results are presented in Proposition B6.<sup>33</sup>

## Proposition B6: The strength of consumer preference and market equilibria

As the strength of consumer preference (??) increases,

(i) The equilibrium changes from Case PP to Case HP if CB11 holds, but changes from Case HP to Case PP if CB12 holds.

(ii) The equilibrium changes from Case PH to Case HH if CB13 holds, but changes from Case HH to Case PH if CB14 holds. The expressions of CB11, CB12, CB13 and CB14 are presented in the detailed proofs below.

Although the role of product fit uncertainty in the impact of the strength of consumer preference on the market equilibrium is not that clear as the main model, we can analyze the results based on identical rationales in the main model.

Consistent with the main model, when the difference in product fit uncertainty between Case HH and Case PH is not taken into consideration, stronger consumer preferences have a greater negative impact on the potential market size of Whole App 1 in Case HH than that in Case PH. Similarly, stronger consumer preferences have a greater negative impact on the potential market size of Whole App 1 in Case PH than that in Case PP.<sup>34</sup> However, offering more free trials in Case HP or Case HH reduces product fit uncertainty, which in turn moderates the difference of the above negative impacts.

Different from that under the freemium strategy, the developer’s profit under the hybrid strategy also depends on the demand of basic app in addition to that of whole app. Stronger consumer preferences have a positive impact on the potential market size of Basic App 1 in Case HP or Case HH, which would be weakened by a larger reduction of product fit uncertainty.

In consequence, if offering more free trials significantly (slightly) reduces product fit uncertainty, an increase in the strength of consumer preference results in a minor (greater) negative impact on the potential market size of Whole App 1, but it also leads to a minor (greater) positive impact on the potential market size of Basic App 1. Therefore, the role of product fit uncertainty in the impact of the strength of consumer preference on the market equilibrium is not that clear as the main model.

Detailed proofs of Proposition B6 are given below:

## Appendix C: Consumers with Learning Costs

## C.1 Equilibrium results for the four possible configurations

## (1) Case PP

Each consumer compares the updated net utilities of the whole apps $( \mathrm { ~ \ E ~ } ( U _ { 1 } ^ { P P } | x _ { P P } ) = v _ { 1 } - t \hat { x } _ { P P } - p _ { 1 } - s$ and $\mathrm { E } ( U _ { 2 } ^ { P P } | x _ { P P } ) = v _ { 2 } - t ( 1 - \hat { x } _ { P P } ) - p _ { 2 } - s )$ to make a decision. By setting $\operatorname { E } ( U _ { 1 } ^ { P P } ) = \operatorname { E } ( U _ { 2 } ^ { P P } )$ , we have the market division point as $\begin{array} { r } { x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } = \frac { t ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) + v _ { 1 } - v _ { 2 } - p _ { 1 } + p _ { 2 } } { 2 t ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) } } \end{array}$ . When $0 < x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } < 1$ and $\mathrm { E } ( U _ { 1 } ^ { P P } | x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } ) \ge 0$ , the market is divided into two segments: the consumers located between 0 and $x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P }$ buy Whole App 1, and those between $x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P }$ and 1 buy Whole $\displaystyle { \bf A p p } \ 2 .$ . The profit functions of Developer 1 and Developer 2 in Case PP are $\pi _ { 1 } = p _ { 1 } x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P }$ and $\pi _ { 2 } = p _ { 2 } ( 1 - x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } )$ respectively. Solving the first order conditions of the profit functions, we obtain the following equilibrium results: $\begin{array} { r } { p _ { 1 } ^ { P P } = \frac { 3 t ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) + v _ { 1 } - v _ { 2 } } { 3 } , p _ { 2 } ^ { P P } = \frac { 3 t ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) - v _ { 1 } + v _ { 2 } } { 3 } , \pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } = \frac { ( 3 t ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) + v _ { 1 } - v _ { 2 } ) ^ { 2 } } { 1 8 t ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) } , \mathrm { a n d } \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P P } = \frac { ( 3 t ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) - v _ { 1 } + v _ { 2 } ) ^ { 2 } } { 1 8 t ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) } , } \end{array}$

## (2) Case PF

The decision process of consumers in Case PF contains two steps. In Step 1, when $\frac { ( \alpha _ { P P } - 2 ) t _ { b } } { 2 } - s + v _ { 2 b } \ge 0$ , each consumer tries the Basic App 2. In Step 2, after trying the basic app, a consumer gets a new signal of misfit. Each consumer compares the updated net utilities of the basic app and whole apps $( \mathrm { E } ( U _ { 1 } ^ { P F } | x _ { P F } ) = v _ { 1 } - t \hat { x } _ { P F } - p _ { 1 } -$ $s , \mathrm { ~ E } ( U _ { 2 } ^ { P F } | x _ { P F } ) = v _ { 2 } - t ( 1 - \hat { x } _ { P F } ) - p _ { 2 }$ and $\mathrm { E } ( U _ { 2 b } ^ { P F } | x _ { P F } ) = v _ { 2 b } - t _ { b } ( 1 - \hat { x } _ { P F } ) )$ to make a decision. By setting $\operatorname { E } ( U _ { 1 } ^ { P F } ) = \operatorname { E } ( U _ { 2 b } ^ { P F } )$ and $\operatorname { E } ( U _ { 2 b } ^ { P F } ) = \operatorname { E } ( U _ { 2 } ^ { P F } )$ we have the market division points as $x _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } =$ $( t + t _ { b } ) ( 1 - \alpha _ { P F } ) - t + \bar { t } _ { b } - 2 s + 2 v _ { 1 } - 2 v _ { 2 b } - 2 \bar { p _ { 1 } }$ and $\begin{array} { r } { x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F } = \frac { ( t - t _ { b } ) ( 1 - \alpha _ { P F } ) + t - t _ { b } - 2 v _ { 2 } + 2 v _ { 2 b } + 2 p _ { 2 } } { 2 ( t - t _ { b } ) ( 1 - \alpha _ { P F } ) } } \end{array}$ . When $0 < x _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } < x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F } < 1$ and 2(??+??<sub>??</sub>)(1−??<sub>????</sub>) $\mathrm { E } ( U _ { 2 b } ^ { P F } | x _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } ) \geq 0$ , the market is divided into three segments: consumers located between 0 and $x _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F }$ buy Whole App 1, and those between $x _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F }$ and $x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F }$ use Basic App 2 for free, and those between $x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F }$ and 1 buy the in-app purchases and use Whole App 2. The profit functions of Developer 1 and Developer 2 in Case PF are $\pi _ { 1 } = p _ { 1 } x _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F }$ and $\pi _ { 2 } =$ $p _ { 2 } ( 1 - x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F } )$ , respectively. ## (3) Case FP

Based on the equilibrium results of Case PF, we can easily derive those of Case FP. Thus, we omit the detailed derivation process and equilibrium results of Case FP.

## Appendix E: Informed Consumers

## E.1 Equilibrium Results for the Four Possible Configurations

## (1) Case PP

By setting $V _ { 1 } - p _ { 1 } = V _ { 2 } - p _ { 2 }$ , we have the market division point as $\begin{array} { r } { y _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } = \frac { t + v _ { 1 } - v _ { 2 } - p _ { 1 } + p _ { 2 } } { 2 t } } \end{array}$ . When $0 < y _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } < 1$ and $V _ { 1 } ( y _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } ) - p _ { 1 } \geq 0$ , the market of informed consumers is divided into two segments: consumers located between 0 and $y _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P }$ buy Whole App 1, and those between $y _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P }$ and 1 buy Whole App 2. When $0 < x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } < 1 , 0 < y _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } < 1$ $\mathrm { E } ( U _ { 1 } ^ { P P } | x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } ) \ge 0$ and $V _ { 1 } ( y _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } ) - p _ { 1 } \geq 0$ , the profit functions of Developer 1 and Developer 2 in Case PP are $\pi _ { 1 } =$ $p _ { 1 } ( \gamma x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } + ( 1 - \gamma ) y _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } )$ and $\pi _ { 2 } = p _ { 2 } ( \gamma ( 1 - x _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } ) + ( 1 - \gamma ) ( 1 - y _ { 1 2 } ^ { P P } ) )$ , respectively. Solving the first order conditions of the profit functions, we obtain the equilibrium results. The equilibrium profits in Case PP are $\pi _ { 1 } ^ { P P } =$ $\begin{array} { r } { \frac { \big ( ( ( v _ { 1 } - v _ { 2 } ) ( 1 - \gamma ) + 3 t ) \alpha _ { P P } - 3 t - v _ { 1 } + v _ { 2 } \big ) ^ { 2 } } { 1 8 \big ( ( \gamma - 1 ) \alpha _ { P P } + 1 \big ) ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) t } \mathrm { ~ a n d ~ } \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P P } = \frac { \big ( \big ( ( v _ { 2 } - v _ { 1 } ) ( 1 - \gamma ) + 3 t \big ) \alpha _ { P P } - 3 t - v _ { 2 } + v _ { 1 } \big ) ^ { 2 } } { 1 8 \big ( ( \gamma - 1 ) \alpha _ { P P } + 1 \big ) ( 1 - \alpha _ { P P } ) t } . } \end{array}$

## (2) Case PF

By setting $V _ { 1 } - p _ { 1 } = V _ { 2 b }$ and $V _ { 2 b } = V _ { 2 } - p _ { 2 }$ , we have the market division points as $\begin{array} { r } { y _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } = \frac { t _ { b } + v _ { 1 } - v _ { 2 b } - p _ { 1 } } { t + t _ { b } } \mathrm { a n d } y _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F } = } \end{array}$ $\frac { t - t _ { b } - { v } _ { 2 } + { v } _ { 2 b } + { p } _ { 2 } } { t - t _ { b } }$ . When $0 < y _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } < y _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F } < 1$ and $V _ { 2 b } ( y _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } ) \ge 0$ , the market of informed consumers is divided into three segments: consumers located between 0 and $y _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F }$ buy Whole App 1, those between $y _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F }$ and $y _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F }$ use Basic App 2 for free, and those between $y _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F }$ and 1 buy the in-app purchases and use Whole App 2. When $0 < x _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } < x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F } < 1 , 0 <$ $y _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } < y _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F } < 1 , \mathrm { E } ( U _ { 2 b } ^ { P F } | \vec { x } _ { 1 2 b } ^ { \vec { P F } } ) \geq 0$ and $V _ { 2 b } ( y _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } ) \ge 0$ , the profit functions of Developer 1 and Developer 2 in Case PF are $\pi _ { 1 } = p _ { 1 } ( \gamma x _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } + ( 1 - \gamma ) y _ { 1 2 b } ^ { P F } )$ and $\pi _ { 2 } = p _ { 2 } ( \gamma ( 1 - x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F } ) + ( 1 - \gamma ) ( 1 - y _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { P F } ) )$ , respectively. Solving the first order conditions of the profit functions, we obtain the equilibrium results. The equilibrium profits in Case PF are $\pi _ { 1 } ^ { P F } =$ $\frac { \big ( ( t - t _ { b } - 2 ( v _ { 1 } - v _ { 2 b } ) ) \gamma \alpha _ { P F } - 2 ( v _ { 1 } - v _ { 2 b } + t _ { b } ) ( 1 - \alpha _ { P F } ) \big ) ^ { 2 } } { 1 6 ( 1 - \alpha _ { P F } ) ( 1 + ( \gamma - 1 ) \alpha _ { P F } ) ( t + t _ { b } ) } \mathrm { a n d } \pi _ { 2 } ^ { P F } = \frac { \big ( ( ( t - t _ { b } - 2 v _ { 2 } + 2 v _ { 2 b } ) \gamma + 2 v _ { 2 } - 2 v _ { 2 b } ) \alpha _ { P F } - 2 v _ { 2 } + 2 v _ { 2 b } \big ) ^ { 2 } } { 1 6 ( 1 - \alpha _ { P F } ) ( 1 + ( \gamma - 1 ) \alpha _ { P F } ) ( t - t _ { b } ) } .$

## (3) Case FP

Based on the equilibrium results of Case PF, we can easily derive those of Case FP. Thus, we omit the detailed derivation process and equilibrium results of Case FP.

## (4) Case FF

By setting $V _ { 1 } - p _ { 1 } = V _ { 1 b } , V _ { 1 b } = V _ { 2 b }$ and $V _ { 2 b } = V _ { 2 } - p _ { 2 }$ , we have the market division points as $\begin{array} { r } { y _ { 1 1 b } ^ { F F } = \frac { v _ { 1 } - v _ { 1 b } - p _ { 1 } } { t - t _ { b } } , } \end{array}$ $\begin{array} { r } { y _ { 1 b 2 b } ^ { F F } = \frac { t _ { b } + v _ { 1 b } - v _ { 2 b } } { 2 t _ { b } } } \end{array}$ and $\begin{array} { r } { y _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { F F } = \frac { t - t _ { b } - v _ { 2 } + v _ { 2 b } + p _ { 2 } } { t - t _ { b } } } \end{array}$ . When $0 < y _ { 1 1 b } ^ { F F } < y _ { 1 b 2 b } ^ { F F } < y _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { F F } < 1$ and $V _ { 1 b } ( y _ { 1 b 2 b } ^ { F F } ) \geq 0$ , the market of informed consumers is divided into four segments: consumers located between 0 and $x _ { 1 1 b } ^ { F F }$ buy the in-app purchases and use Whole App 1, those between $x _ { 1 1 b } ^ { F F }$ and $x _ { 1 b 2 b } ^ { F F }$ use Basic $\mathsf { A p p }$ 1 for free, and those between $x _ { 1 b 2 b } ^ { F F }$ and $x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { F F }$ use Basic $\mathrm { A p p } 2$ for free, and those between $x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { F F }$ and 1 buy the in-app purchases and use Whole App 2. When $0 < x _ { 1 1 b } ^ { F F } <$ $x _ { 1 b 2 b } ^ { F F } < \bar { x } _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { F F } < 1 , 0 < y _ { 1 1 b } ^ { F F } < y _ { 1 b 2 b } ^ { F F } < \bar { y } _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { F F } < 1 , \mathrm { E } ( U _ { 1 b } ^ { F F } | x _ { 1 b 2 b } ^ { F F } ) \geq 0$ and $V _ { 1 b } ( y _ { 1 b 2 b } ^ { F F } ) \geq 0$ , the profit functions of Developer 1 and Developer 2 in Case FF are $\pi _ { 1 } = p _ { 1 } ( \gamma x _ { 1 1 b } ^ { F F } + ( 1 - \gamma ) y _ { 1 1 b } ^ { F F } )$ and $\pi _ { 2 } = p _ { 2 } ( \gamma ( 1 - x _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { F F } ) + ( 1 - \gamma ) ( 1 -$ $y _ { 2 b 2 } ^ { F F } ) )$ , respectively. Solving the first order conditions of the profit functions, we obtain the equilibrium results. ## About the Authors

Xiaoxiao Luo is an assistant professor in the School of Business Administration, Faculty of Business Administration, Southwestern University of Finance and Economics, China. She received her doctoral degree in management science and engineering from the College of Management and Economics, Tianjin University. Her research interests include pricing information goods and platform business models. Her recent papers have appeared in Computers & Industrial Engineering and Asia-Pacific Journal of Operational Research.

Jie Zhang is a professor in the Department of Information Systems and Operation Management at the University of Texas at Arlington. She received her doctoral degree in computer information systems from the Simon School of Business Administration, University of Rochester. Her research covers a wide spectrum of areas, including channel management, information goods, electronic commerce, social media, etc. Her research papers have been published in MIS Quarterly, Information Systems Research, Journal of Management Information Systems, Journal of Economics and Management Strategies, Communications of the ACM, Decision Support System, etc.

Minqiang Li is a professor in the Department of Information Management and Management Science, College of Management and Economics, Tianjin University, China. He received a PhD in management sciences from Tianjin University. His research interests cover management science and decision support, ICT strategy, e-commerce, data mining and business intelligence, and evolutionary computation. His papers have appeared in MIS Quarterly, Management Science, Journal of the Association for Information Systems, Journal of Management Information Systems, European Journal of Operational Research, Journal of Evolutionary Economics, IEEE Transactions on Neural Networks and Learning Systems, and other venues.
